| SG-39 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-39) — awaiting owner re-judgement | **The yaw/roll collapse was mechanical and is fixed at the root; typed input is on every numeric row; and the friendliness pass landed — kept OPEN in the ledger for the owner's hands-on verdict, like the crate.** **MEASURED first:** at pitch −90 the stored Euler yaw and roll build the SAME basis to **1.6e-8** (`Basis.from_euler(−90,10,0)` vs `(−90,0,10)`, order YXZ), so the owner's "they do the same thing" is literally one control, not perception — the shipped cutlass fit sits at −96 on that singularity. **PRIORITY 1 — the gimbal collapse.** The mount's live rotation is now a **Basis**, not three Euler fields. Every rotation nudge — the PITCH/YAW/ROLL buttons, the A/D W/S Q/E keys, the wheel over a row, and right-drag — composes a turn about the weapon's OWN live local axis (`LabMath.rotate_local`: `basis.rotated(basis*axis, θ).orthonormalized()`), never an Euler increment, so the three rows are always distinct (measured **0.59** apart at the same pitch that collapsed to 1.6e-8). **File stays Euler** (`rotation:[x,y,z]` degrees, extracted at save via `Basis.get_euler(YXZ)` — the order `Node3D.rotation`/`hold()` applies, pinned by a check); a load that isn't rotated re-saves byte-identical numbers (the euler array is only re-extracted once you actually turn it). **FIT-COMPATIBILITY GUARANTEE:** the shipped fit loads to the same visible pose before and after — the check `lab · the shipped weapons.json fit loads to the same basis after the local-axis refactor` asserts `basis_drift(old, load→extract→reload) < 1e-4` on the real weapons.json rotation (measured **5e-7**). **PRIORITY 2 — typed input everywhere.** ONE reused `LineEdit` (`_make_value`/`_begin_edit`/`_commit_edit`) serves all rows — MOUNT's seven, the four LIGHT rows, the nine FX dials: click the value → type → ENTER applies (clamped to the row's range; rotation sets an absolute Euler component and rebuilds the basis), ESC or click-away cancels, malformed input refused and the old value kept (`LabMath.parse_number` via `is_valid_float`). **PRIORITY 3 — friendliness (all done):** hovering or nudging a rotation row lights the matching coloured local axis ON THE BLADE (weapon axes drawn in `_draw_overlay`, X red/pitch · Y green/yaw · Z blue/roll, active bright) so you SEE which line spins; Shift = step ×10, Alt = ×0.1, both shown in the header via `_step_hint`; wheel over a row nudges it (`_row_scroll`); the dev-note instruction block rewritten to a plain hint per mode; the "renderer has no size for this" fragment reworded to read as information; single-level **Ctrl+Z** undo of the last nudge/typed entry (`_snapshot`/`_undo`). Layout untouched — the owner LIKES the recent changes. **TESTABLE LOGIC pulled OUT of the windowed tool** (SG-29: the lab's readback hangs headless) into `tools/lab_math.gd` (`class_name LabMath`), which the harness loads and asserts directly. **CHECK STRINGS (7 new):** `lab · the fit Euler order is Node3D's own default, so hold() reproduces the basis` (YXZ==2), `lab · the shipped weapons.json fit loads to the same basis after the local-axis refactor` (drift 5e-7), `lab · at pitch -90 the Euler yaw and roll rows collapse to one pose — the reported bug` (1.6e-8), `lab · but a local-axis nudge about pitch, yaw and roll stays distinct at that singularity` (closest pair 0.59), `lab · a local-axis rotation nudge changes the pose`, `lab · the typed-input parser accepts a well-formed number and reads its value`, `lab · and refuses malformed input so the old value is kept`. **VERIFY:** harness **510/510** green (503 baseline + 7); the lab boots WINDOWED clean in MOUNT and FX with zero script errors/warnings (headless screenshot readback skipped per SG-29 — the interaction is described here for hand-verification). | SG-34 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-34) | **MEASURED FIRST, and the premise was refuted — then fixed the fault the measurement actually found.** The board said the Godot deck read dark, cool, vignetted, edges to near-black blue. A PIL probe over the six `.shots/parity/` stitches (HUD-masked deck region, browser LEFT = reference) found the OPPOSITE on every scene: Godot was BRIGHTER (deck luminance ~53 vs the browser's ~44), WARMER (mean R/B ~1.75 vs ~1.49), warmer in the surround, LESS cool (bluish-pixel fraction ~7% vs ~18%) and FLATTER (contrast 0.49 vs 0.66) than the browser — the browser is the darker, cooler, moodier image. The one real fault both eye and probe agreed on: a searing HOT POOL (99th-pct luminance 163 vs 148, blown-highlight fraction up to 0.75 vs 0.54) that by simultaneous contrast made the warm surround *read* cold, over a too-bright, washed deck that had lost the browser's deep amber. The "blue-black crates" were measured warm at the faces (R/B 2.4–2.7) and read cool only against the pool — the sole genuinely-cool object is the `crate_stack` MODEL texture (stayed navy when the cool key was cut 1.45→1.15, so not the lighting — filed SG-41). **FIX in `scripts/view3d.gd` (environment/lights):** deliberate exposure `1.0 → 0.80` (deepen the whole frame to the browser's richer amber); calm the accent pools — boiler-glow omni `0.45+1.25·life → 0.38+0.95·life`, brazier/lantern omnis `1.5/1.0 → 1.2/0.82`, painted floor-pool alpha `0.34/0.24 → 0.26/0.18`, and the primitive-fallback furnace emission `2.6 → 2.0` (the live mesh emission is already 1.0); ambient re-set from cool-purple `#4a4058` to a near-neutral `#494551` (a FAINT cool fill kept for depth, not a strong cast) and fog `#3a2f4a → #3a3340`; the cool moon KEY kept at 1.45 — measured, cutting it only spiked the deck orange (R/B 2.3) and fixed no crate. **Mid-flight the owner retired parity** ("better than the web one ever was") — so the target was reframed as warm/even/legible with the depth a flat Canvas 2D never had, browser as reference not spec. **BEFORE → AFTER → BROWSER (deck-region, HUD-masked; deck / effects representative):** luminance 53→44 (browser 44) · warmth 1.77→1.88 (1.49) · surround-warmth 1.51→1.62 (1.34) · cool% 7→5.6 (17) · contrast 0.50→0.54 (0.66) · p99 163→150 (148) · hi% 0.75→0.35 (0.54). All four measured scenes converge the same way: deepened to browser luminance, hot pool calmed to browser p99, warm and even, a touch warmer/flatter than the browser by intent. Telegraphs/element flashes pop HARDER on the deepened deck (I darkened, not brightened — the task's "brighter deck washes a decal" risk inverted), element light-decay signatures untouched (`impact_at` not edited). **GUARD (1 new check, 502→503):** `view · the deck is lit warm and even, not a hot pool` reads the built `view._environment` + `_moon`/`_lantern` and asserts the shipped constants — Filmic tonemap, exposure in `[0.70,0.92]` (can't return to the washed 1.0), ambient a near-neutral fill (`b−r ≤ 0.05`, no strong cool cast) at energy `[0.45,0.80]`, the cool moon a key not a flood (`1.0 ≤ e ≤ 1.6`, still blue), a warm lantern fill floor present, and the boiler furnace emission `≤ 2.3` (not the old 2.6 sear). Constants not a frame readback (SG-29). **VERIFY:** harness **503/503**; all six stitches regenerated (`python tools/parity.py`), `.shots/parity/{deck,fight,effects,boss,hud,draft}.png`. | SG-16 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-16) — DELETED | **Verdict: DELETE the field, not honour it.** `ELEMENT_FX[*].life` (EMBER 0.70, FROST 0.28, ARC 0.22, STEAM 0.95) was declared per element and NOTHING read it — the emitter's own `lifetime = 1.0` governed every family, so it was per-element timing that never rendered (failure mode one, found by the lab). **Why delete, not honour:** honouring a per-PARTICLE lifetime needs one emitter per element, and rendering-audit finding 3 (DESIGN §13m) is emphatic that emitters are keyed by BEHAVIOUR (spark/shard/steam), colour riding on the particle, because a shared `emit_particle` emitter that is never restarted is the fix to a real bug — per-element emitters would regress that, break the pinned `impact · one emitter per behaviour, not one per element` check, and add a 512-cap node against §13m's still-open pool budgets. It could not even be honoured per FAMILY: EMBER (0.70) and ARC (0.22) share the `spark` emitter with different lifetimes, so the field was self-contradictory as per-element data on a per-behaviour system — proof it was never wireable. **The §13m finding-4 signature survives intact WITHOUT it:** the timing half is the LIGHT decay (26/s FROST·ARC vs 8/s EMBER·STEAM, hardcoded in `impact_at`, tested green — `impact · and their light dies at different rates` frost 26/s ember 8/s), the motion half is rise/spread/speed (tested — `impact · elements differ in motion, not only in colour`, which used to lean on `.life` and now leans on `frost.speed > ember.speed`). Reasoning recorded at the deletion site (the `ELEMENT_FX` const comment). **GUARD (1 new check, the twin of the talent-field/article guards):** `impact · every ELEMENT_FX field is read by the renderer` greps `view3d.gd` for every key on every entry — the table cannot grow another dead field. **Harness 462→463.** Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd` (own hunks). Commit: see git log for SG-16. |
| SG-10 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-10) | **The second deckwork verb shipped: HEAVE THE CRATE.** One pass, one verb, complete — sim, discoverability, rules, checks — per the repair pattern. **What it is:** a movable cargo stack (`crates` prop, so it is mirrored into 3D with NO renderer change) the captain heaves across the PORT lane to funnel the boarders walking down it. Held on the same `deckwork` key as repair (`SkyGearKeybinds`), context-sensitive: stand at the crate and the generic HUD prompt reads `HOLD R · HEAVE THE CRATE` with the live binding, a progress bar under it, and `— BOARDERS ARE ON IT —` when contested — all reused from repair, which is the whole point of the table. **Rules as shipped:** (1) COST is TIME and only time — `HEAVE_SECONDS 2.8` out of the fight per heave (heavier than repair's 2.4; a loaded stack is heavy), the sharpest cost the deck has; no new currency, honouring deckwork.gd's doctrine. (2) BALANCE is GEOMETRY — one 150-wide crate against a 380-wide lane band NARROWS a lane and pins the fight to one side, but can never wall it shut for free; the outboard side always stays open (harness-proven). A heave cycles stow→narrow→funnel→stow, so the same key also RETRACTS it. (3) PERSISTENCE: the crate RE-STOWS to its bow home every wave with the rest of the ordnance (the pinned deck re-stow), BY DESIGN — a flank you closed is one you pay seconds to close again next wave, never a permanent free wall, and legible because the crate visibly returns home each wave. The pinned `deck · the deck is re-stowed for the next wave` check stays green (31 standing), NOT silenced. (4) PATHING responds for real: `_funnel_past_crate` ejects a boarder from the crate's footprint to the nearer OPEN side (never toward the stern) inside `correct_enemy_position` — the three lines boarders actually path by — so the funnel is true, not painted (STATUS failure mode one). **ONE source of truth for cargo:** new `game.cargo_rects()` (the 8 fixed walls + the live crate) now feeds `correct_player_position`, the funnel and the 2D debug draw; the crate footprint is derived from the prop the player SEES, so what blocks and what is drawn are one object (STATUS failure mode two). **Coach:** new `shape_lane` hint announces the otherwise-invisible verb while a lane is walking through and the crate sits stowed, carrying the live binding via the same `{key}` substitution as `broken_cannon`. **Check strings (11 new, harness 446→457):** `deck · the deck carries a movable crate to heave`, `deck · the crate joins the one cargo source of truth`, `deck · standing at the crate offers a heave`, `deck · the heave verb has a prompt and a reason it refuses`, `deck · heaving actually moves the crate's footprint`, `deck · a boarder in the port lane is funnelled by the heaved crate`, `deck · and the open side of the funnelled lane still passes`, `deck · and stowing the crate re-opens that column`, `deck · the crate re-stows to its home for the next wave`, `coach · the crate's heave is announced with a lane walking through`, `coach · and the heave line names the bound key, not the {key} token` (asserts the live binding `R`, not the raw `{key}`). **Verify:** harness **457/457** green; text audit windowed CONTAINMENT clean, LEGIBILITY `0 faint · 0 small`. | SG-27 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-27) | **MEASURED, then rescaled — verdict (a), keep the mesh at the browser's height; verdict (b) rejected because the "flat fallback" it names is a myth.** `tools/boiler_measure.gd` (new) poses `main3d.tscn` at the deck framing and measures the ACTUAL rendered Boiler subtree, unprojected through the real camera. **Before:** the generated mesh, scaled to `BOILER_HEIGHT=168` (a taste number, "buys the pipes"), rendered a near-cube — **168 tall × 167 wide × 162 DEEP** ground units, **208.6 px** tall / **290.8 px** wide at 1920×1080 — a solid drum dominating the lower third against the browser's FLAT **150-unit / 183.6 px** block. That is the boarding-hulk failure mode (a mesh as deep as it is wide) and it, not the camera (SG-2), was the "everything looks bigger" residual. **The `boilerH:132` in DESIGN §13c / this board's old title is STALE** — that was the v3/v4 preset; the LIVE build `parity.py` photographs is **storm-dusk-v11, `boilerH:150`**, so 150 is the real target. **Decision:** the primitive fallback that verdict (b) would fall back to is NOT the flat engine block §13c describes — measured it renders 178×**192×192** gu / 222.9 px, a smooth golden ball, CHUNKIER than the mesh (filed SG-30). The mesh's furnace face is authored to +Z, the one direction this locked 41° camera ever sees, so — unlike the hulk's hidden ramp — it reads as the furnace square-on. So: kept the mesh, set `BOILER_HEIGHT 168 → 150` (`scripts/view3d.gd`), pinning the render to the browser's own `boilerH`. **After:** rendered Boiler **150.0 tall × 148.8 × 144.6** gu, **183.6 px** tall = the browser's 183.6 px exactly (ratio **1.000**), width 290.8→259.7 px; the captain (spawns 130u in front, crown 128u) stands clearly clear of it at run start — §13c regression test held, confirmed in `.shots/parity/{deck,fight}.png`. **GUARD (check string):** `boiler · renders at the browser's boilerH, not a taller mesh's own height` (reads the live subtree AABB = 150.0 gu, asserts it and `BOILER_HEIGHT` both equal browser boilerH 150 ±3) — a generated mesh cannot silently regress this again. **Harness 446/446** (baseline 445 + 1). Evidence regenerated: `.shots/parity/{deck,fight,effects,boss}.png`. Files: `scripts/view3d.gd`, `tests/parity_test.gd` (own hunk only), `tools/boiler_measure.gd` (new), `docs/BOARD.md`. It was the DOWNSCALE.** `ink.gd` chose `MIN_PT=12` to "survive the 0.83 downscale of a 1600 window" and explicitly nothing narrower, but nothing STOPPED the window going narrower — and the HUD is a full-rect Control in a CanvasLayer under `canvas_items`, so the 1920 layout never reflows, it just scales to the window. **Measured before touching anything** (new `tools/legibility_probe.gd`, real 1920 layout scaled per width): at a 1280 window **340 of 627 HUD strings render at 8.0 physical px** — every 12pt label, the exact sub-pixel-stem smudge `MIN_PT` exists to prevent — across the owner's named surfaces: skill/binding labels (`V BLOW`,`F MAIN`,`SPC JET`), lane labels (`PORT`/`CENTRE`/`STARBOARD`), boiler/wave readouts, card titles, draft before/after values, workshop labels. **Contrast was already clean and stays clean** (full text audit, windowed): `0 faint · 0 small`, bare-plate ratios as low as 2.52 on textured brass but the 2px outline lifts effective contrast to 8.8–17 — **no tint changed**. **Fix (minimum intervention, zero layout change):** enforce `get_window().min_size = 1600×900` in `game.gd._ready` (formalises the project's own default windowed size), so the 0.83× downscale MIN_PT was calibrated for is the worst case; single source of truth added to `ink.gd` — `BASE_W=1920`, `MIN_WINDOW_W=1600`, `MIN_PHYS_PX=10.0`, `physical_pt()`. **After:** probe reports **0 of 627 strings under 10px** at the enforced min window (worst is exactly 10.0px); `tools/text_audit.gd` SMALL now means "under `MIN_PHYS_PX` physical px at the min window" (was a dead `pt<MIN_PT` test the funnel could never trip) with a new `PHYS` column, plus `tools/legibility_probe.gd`. **Verify:** harness **445/445** green (2 new ink checks, 0 failures, nothing silenced); full text audit windowed — CONTAINMENT clean, LEGIBILITY `0 faint · 0 small`, exit 0; gallery `.shots/screens/after/index.html` (84 shots). | SG-1 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-1) | Check `class · but ground covered against a dashing captain is worse than that` was nondeterministic, not machine-specific: it read `global_position` after `move_and_slide()`, which steps by the engine's real-frame physics delta (`get_physics_process_delta_time()`), not the loop's 0.05 sim tick — so it measured wall-clock jitter (200–570% run to run; 33% and 180% were two draws), compounded by the wave auto-completing and flipping state to DRAFT, which disabled `controls_enabled` mid-measurement. Fix: integrate `\|velocity\| * 0.05` (the speed the sim deterministically produces) and force `controls_enabled` on each tick. Now bit-for-bit repeatable — captain 3076, boilerwright 1228 every run — reading **40%** (was flapping). Harness 437/437, three consecutive green runs. Corrected the ledger's 67%/33% mobility gap to 60%/40% (`OUTSTANDING.md`). Fix is this commit (see `git log` for SG-1). |
| SG-3 | DONE 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-3) | **The melee windup was drawn, but as a plank-wide streak plus a small foot ring — measured against the browser it was a fraction of the readable danger.** The browser (`_render_entities.js drawTelegraphs`) draws a MELEE windup as a filled `PAL.danger` #FF3D2E wedge covering the swing arc out to `reach` (SCRAPPER 92/95°, ARMORED 118/120°, SWARM 64/80° — `_core_patched.js`), inner `PAL.dangerIn` #FF8C1A fill growing as the wind completes; lead time = `windup` (0.40/0.55/0.40 s); RANGED = a danger band + dashed aim line of `atkRange+80`. The port's `reach`/`swing` were never ported — it faked reach with a flat `attack_range + 24..28` fudge and drew a thin streak. **Fixed:** ported `reach`/`swing` into `game_data.gd` (the browser's tuned values), made the sim connect at `reach + target_radius` (`enemy.gd`) so what is DRAWN and what CONNECTS are ONE number (STATUS failure mode two), rebuilt the telegraph in `view3d.gd` as a filled danger wedge (`_fan_texture`) with a growing inner clock, and gave the GUNNER a bright firing band with a counting-down hot core. Evidence: harness `445/445` green; new checks `telegraph · a windup appends a telegraph decal to the deck`, `telegraph · the wedge is drawn at the reach the swing connects at` (drew 92 vs reach 92), `telegraph · every melee windup has a reach and a swing arc to draw`, `telegraph · a ranged shooter is not handed a melee swing`, `telegraph · the windup gives at least the browser's warning time`, `telegraph · the windup pool stays inside its reserve under flood` (48/48); `lanes · a boarder in every lane can reach the Boiler` still green on the new reach. Posed before/after in `.shots/telegraph/{before,after}/telegraphs.png` (`tools/telegraph_shot.gd`). Ranged dashed-aim-line polish filed as SG-28. Commit: see git log for SG-3. |
| SG-2 | CORRECTED 2026-08-01 (opus/SG-2) | **The camera was ported exactly; there is no framing gap. Measured, not asserted** (`tools/cam_measure.gd`): at one output resolution the browser's own `CAM.project()` and Godot's `Camera3D.unproject_position` agree to the pixel on every known length — deck full width 1680 = **3288.1 px in BOTH** (ratio 1.000); at the bow 1137.5 both; one lane 560 = 1096.0 both; vertical bow→stern 1695.7 both; the captain's ground point lands at the identical pixel (960, 705.4). Root cause of the false "zoomed in" finding: the browser's `_f = f * View.unit` (`_render_head.js:53`) scales focal length with resolution exactly as Godot's fixed vertical FOV does, so at any 16:9 aspect the two projections are identical — no `WORLD_SCALE`, deck-rect, distance or FOV gap exists. The original impression was made against a broken browser render (procedural sky fallback, images blocked over `file://`, as `parity.py`'s own comment documents) and/or unmatched resolution. Fresh evidence with the real art in `.shots/parity/{deck,fight,effects,boss}.png`; the one real residual (the Boiler PROP mesh, not the camera) filed as SG-27. Harness 437/437 — camera checks `the lens is the browser's focal length` (36.09°) and `the captain stands where the art was framed for` (0.600) both green, asserting the confirmed-correct invariant. |
